Generalized Multiple Operator Integrals for Operators with Finite Dimensions
Shih-Yu Chang

Abstract
Multiple Operator Integrals (MOIs) have played a foundational role in operator theory and functional calculus, particularly for analyzing Hermitian matrices via spectral decomposition. Conventional MOIs rely on the assumption of self-adjointness, making them analytically tractable for computing Fréchet derivatives, establishing trace formulas, and deriving commutator estimates. However, many problems in mathematics, science, and engineering involve matrices that are fundamentally non-Hermitian, arising in contexts such as numerical discretization of differential operators, signal processing, control systems, and non-Hermitian physics. These cases necessitate a more general framework for operator integration. In this paper, we propose and develop the theory of Generalized Multiple Operator Integrals (GMOIs) , which extends MOI techniques to arbitrary matrices, including non-Hermitian and non-normal cases. We unify conventional MOIs under the perspective of the Spectral Mapping Theorem and present a rigorous construction of Generalized Double Operator Integrals (GDOIs) in finite-dimensional setting via Jordan decomposition of any matrix. We establish key algebraic properties, derive norm estimates, and prove continuity and Lipschitz-type perturbation formulas. Finally, we demonstrate how GMOIs can be used to compute matrix function derivatives in non-Hermitian environments, thus significantly broadening the applicability of MOI-based methods in both theoretical and practical domains.



Index terms—
Multiple Operator Integral (MOI), spectral mapping theorem, perturbation formula, Hermitian matrix, Jordan decomposition.

1
Introduction

In the study of functional calculus and operator theory, Multiple Operator Integrals (MOIs) have emerged as effective tools for analyzing the properties of operator functions, mainly in perturbation theory and non-commutative analysis
[
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
]
. The conventional method of MOIs is basically grounded from the spectral concept of Hermitian (self-adjoint) matrices, where the spectral decomposition permits the representation of operator functions through integration over spectral projections. In this framework, functions with several argument Hermitian matrices are expressed as iterated integrals involving spectral measures associated with each operator, and kernel functions are used to model the interaction among eigenvalues from input matrices. This conventional MOI method plays an important position in deriving high-order Fréchet derivatives, establishing trace formulae, and facilitating the analysis of commutator estimates. The assumption of Hermitian structure guarantees properly-described spectral projections and facilitates analytical tractability, making it a cornerstone in the theoretical basis of present day perturbation analysis in matrix and operator theory.

While Hermitian matrices play an important function in lots of areas of mathematics and quantum physics due to their real eigenvalues and orthogonal eigenvectors, a wide variety of programs throughout mathematics, science, and engineering involve matrices which are fundamentally non-Hermitian. These non-Hermitian matrices, which do not satisfy the situation
\bm{A}=\bm{A}^{\mathrm{H}}
, where
\mathrm{H}
is the Hermitian operation, rise up naturally in contexts in which dissipation, asymmetry, or directionality is intrinsic to the system. In the field of numerical linear algebra and differential equations, the discretization of non-self-adjoint differential operators (which includes convection-diffusion operators) ends in non-Hermitian matrices
[
5
]
. These operators lack the symmetry necessary to guarantee real spectra, posing unique challenges in spectral analysis and numerical stability.

In engineering fields, non-Hermitian matrices frequently seem in control principle, signal processing, and communication dynamics. Systems with feedback delay, non-reciprocal transmission, or anisotropic fabric residences regularly yield non-Hermitian gadget matrices. In particular, the modeling of open or lossy physical systems in optics and electromagnetics, inclusive of in emph non-Hermitian photonics, calls for complicated-symmetric or even complicated-nonsymmetric matrices to explain energy alternate and mode coupling
[
6
]
. Furthermore, in carrying out physics and biology, non-Hermitian Hamiltonians are used to explain systems with gain and loss or irreversible procedures, together with population dynamics and neural networks
[
7
]
. As such, the study and application of non-Hermitian matrices are both theoretically wealthy and almost quintessential in many contemporary scientific and engineering fields.

Conventional formulations of Multiple Operator Integrals (MOIs) should rely on the spectral decomposition of Hermitian matrices, restricting their applicability to cases with non Hermitian matrices. However, many practical and theoretical problems in modern analysis, physics, and engineering involve operators or matrices that are not Hermitian. To overcome this limitation, we proposed a framework of
Generalized MOIs
, which extend the theory to arbitrary matrices—including those that are non-Hermitian or even non-normal. This extension preserves the integral representation structure while accounting for the broader spectral behavior of such matrices. In the finite-dimensional context, we developed a rigorous construction for Generalized Double Operator Integrals (GDOIs) based on matrix decomposition techniques and Jordan canonical forms
[
8
]
. Moreover, in infinite-dimensional settings involving operators with continuous spectra, we further generalized the integral framework to accommodate spectral measures beyond the discrete case, offering new tools for functional calculus in non-self-adjoint operator theory
[
9
]
. These developments open the door for applying MOI-based techniques in diverse applications, including non-Hermitian perturbation theory, control theory, and quantum systems with complex spectra.

The primary contributions of this paper are as follows. First, we establish that conventional MOIs for Hermitian matrices can be unified under the perspective of the Spectral Mapping Theorem, offering a conceptual bridge to more general formulations. We then introduce the novel framework of Generalized Multiple Operator Integrals (GMOIs), which extends the applicability of MOIs to arbitrary square matrices, including non-Hermitian and non-normal cases. This generalization is accompanied by a thorough investigation of their algebraic and analytic properties. We derive explicit norm estimates for GMOIs, providing essential tools for their boundedness and stability analysis. Furthermore, we develop a perturbation formula and corresponding Lipschitz estimates, enabling controlled sensitivity analysis of operator functions under matrix perturbations. The continuity properties of GMOIs are rigorously characterized, ensuring their compatibility with continuous spectral data. Lastly, we illustrate the effectiveness of our framework by applying the perturbation formula to compute derivatives of matrix functions in terms of GMOIs, thereby extending the scope of matrix calculus in finite-dimensional and non-Hermitian settings.

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we revisit classical Multiple Operator Integrals (MOIs) and demonstrate that they can be understood as specific instances of the Spectral Mapping Theorem applied to Hermitian matrices. Section
3
introduces the framework of Generalized Multiple Operator Integrals (GMOIs) applicable to general, possibly non-Hermitian matrices, and explores their fundamental algebraic structures. Section
4
is devoted to the derivation of norm estimates for GMOIs, establishing rigorous bounds that are essential for perturbative analysis. In Section
5
, we develop a perturbation formula for GMOIs and derive corresponding Lipschitz-type continuity estimates. Section
6
examines the continuity properties of GMOIs with respect to parameter matrix perturbations, ensuring the robustness of the framework under small variations. Finally, in Section
7
, we demonstrate the utility of the proposed theory by applying the perturbation formula to derive differentiation results for matrix-valued functions.

2
Conventional MOIs are Special Cases of Spectral Mapping Theorem

Let us review conventional DOI definitions. Given a function
\beta:\mathbb{R}^{\zeta+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
\zeta+1

\zeta

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k_{1}}}\lambda_{k_{1}}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}};

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k_{2}}}\lambda_{k_{2}}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}};

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k_{\zeta+1}}}\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}}\bm{P}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}},

Example 1

Let us consider a triple operator integral
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})
with Hermitian matrices
\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}
and
\bm{X}_{3}
. Then, we can express
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})
as

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})

(11)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{1}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}+\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{2}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}+\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{1}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{2}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{1}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{2}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

If we set the function
f
in Theorem
1
by

\displaystyle f(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4},z_{5})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta(z_{1},z_{3},\,z_{5})z_{2}z_{4},

(12)

and set

\displaystyle z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{k_{1}},\penalty\ \penalty\ z_{3}\penalty\ =\penalty\ \lambda_{k_{2}},\penalty\ \penalty\ z_{5}\penalty\ =\penalty\ \lambda_{k_{3}},

\displaystyle z_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{1}},\penalty\ \penalty\ z_{4}\penalty\ =\penalty\ \lambda_{k^{\prime}_{2}},

(13)

then, by applying Theorem
1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4},z_{5})}{\partial z_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta(z_{1},z_{3},\,z_{5})z_{4};

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4},z_{5})}{\partial z_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta(z_{1},z_{3},\,z_{5})z_{2};

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4},z_{5})}{\partial z_{2}\partial z_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta(z_{1},z_{3},\,z_{5}).

(14)

Therefore, we obtain:

\displaystyle f(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{Y}_{2},\bm{X}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{1}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{2}}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

(15)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{1}}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\lambda_{k^{\prime}_{2}}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{P}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{1}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{K^{\prime}_{2}}\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{\alpha^{\mathrm{G}}_{k^{\prime}_{2}}}\bm{N}_{k^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}}\right)\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}.

By comparing Eq. (
11
) and Eq. (
15
), we have

\displaystyle f(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{Y}_{2},\bm{X}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2}).

(16)

3
Generalized Multiple Operator Integrals and Their Algebraic Properties

We begin by defining the GMOIs in Section
3.1
, followed by an exploration of the algebraic properties of MOIs in Section
3.2
.

3.1
Generalized Multiple Operator Integrals

According to Theorem
1
, GMOIs can be defined as follows.

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})

(17)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\ldots\bm{Y}_{\zeta}\bm{P}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{\kappa=1}^{\zeta}\sum\limits_{\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})}\Bigg(\sum\limits_{\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1}))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1}))}}-1}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{\beta^{\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{q_{\iota_{1}}!q_{\iota_{2}}!\ldots q_{\iota_{\kappa}}!}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{r})),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{r})),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{P}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{q_{1}=\ldots=q_{\zeta+1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1,\ldots,m_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{q_{1}!\cdots q_{\zeta+1}!}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \times\bm{N}^{q_{1}}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}^{q_{2}}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\ldots\bm{Y}_{\zeta}\bm{N}^{q_{\zeta+1}}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}},

where
\bm{Y}_{\zeta+1}
is set as identity matrix.

3.2
GMOI Algebraic Properties

In this section, we will establish the algebraic properties of the operator
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
defined by Eq. (
17
).

If the matrices
\bm{X}_{p}
for
p=1,2,\ldots,\zeta+1
are decomposed as:

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\lambda_{k_{p}}\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}}+\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\bm{N}_{k_{p},i_{p}}

(18)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{p,\bm{P}}+\bm{X}_{p,\bm{N}},

then, from the definition of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
provided in Eq. (
17
), we have the following decomposition proposition with respect to parameters matrices
\bm{X}_{p}
immediately.

Proposition 1

Given matrices
\bm{X}_{p}
, which are decomposed as Eq. (
18
), respectively, then, we have

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2^{\zeta+1}}T_{\beta}^{[\bm{X}]_{\xi(i)}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}),

(19)

where
[\bm{X}]_{\xi(i)}
is an array with
\zeta+1
entries such that its
j
-th entry is expressed by

\displaystyle([\bm{X}]_{\xi(i)})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{X}_{j,P},&\text{if}\left\lfloor\frac{i-1}{2^{j-1}}\right\rfloor\equiv 0\pmod{2};\\
\bm{X}_{j,N},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(20)

For example, if we consider
\zeta=2
, we have GTOI as below:

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}

(21)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(-,-,q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{3}!}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(-,q_{2},-)}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{2}!}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},-,-)}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!}\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(-,q_{2},q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{2}!q_{3}!}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},-,q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!q_{3}!}\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},q_{2},-)}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!q_{2}!}\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!q_{2}!q_{3}!}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \times\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2^{3}}T_{\beta}^{[\bm{X}]_{\xi(i)}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2}),

where
=_{1}
comes from Proposition
1
.

In GDOI, the composition of two GDOIs is still a GDOI. However, for GMOI, the composition of a GMOI with multiple GMOIs will change the number of parameter matrices for the composed GMOI. We have the following Propotion
2
to characterize such composition behavior.

Proposition 2

Given the following GMOIs:

\displaystyle T_{f}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}),

(22)

and

\displaystyle T_{\beta_{i}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}),

(23)

where
i=1,2,\ldots,\zeta
and
[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1}
is an array of matrices given by
[\bm{Y}]_{1}^{\zeta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}
. If the matrices
\bm{X}_{p}
for
p=1,2,\ldots,\zeta+1
are decomposed as:

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\lambda_{k_{p}}\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}}+\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\bm{N}_{k_{p},i_{p}}.

(24)

Then, we have

\displaystyle T_{f\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\zeta}\beta_{i}}^{\bm{X}_{1},[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\bm{X}_{2},[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\ldots,[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\overbrace{\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I},\ldots,\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I}}^{\mbox{there are $\zeta$ terms of $\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I}$}})

(25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{f}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(T_{\beta_{1}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}),\ldots,T_{\beta_{\zeta}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})),

where
[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}
is an array of matrices given by
[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}
. Note that there are
(\zeta+1)^{2}
parameter matrices in GMOI given by L.H.S. of Eq. (
25
)

Proof:

From GMOI definition given by Eq. (
17
), we have

\displaystyle T_{f}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(T_{\beta_{1}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}),\ldots,T_{\beta_{\zeta}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta}))

(26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}f(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}T_{\beta_{1}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \times\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}T_{\beta_{2}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\ldots T_{\beta_{\zeta}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\bm{P}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{\kappa=1}^{\zeta}\sum\limits_{\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})}\Bigg(\sum\limits_{\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1}))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1}))}}-1}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{f^{\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{q_{\iota_{1}}!q_{\iota_{2}}!\ldots q_{\iota_{\kappa}}!}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{r})),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}T_{\beta_{\varsigma}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\varrho_{\kappa}(q_{1},\ldots,q_{r})),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{P}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}T_{\beta_{\varsigma}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{q_{1}=\ldots=q_{\zeta+1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1,\ldots,m_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}-1}\frac{f^{(q_{1},\ldots,q_{\zeta+1})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{q_{1}!\cdots q_{\zeta+1}!}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \times\bm{N}^{q_{1}}_{k_{1},i_{1}}T_{\beta_{1}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\bm{N}^{q_{2}}_{k_{2},i_{2}}T_{\beta_{2}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\ldots

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \times\bm{N}^{q_{\zeta}}_{k_{\zeta},i_{\zeta}}T_{\beta_{\zeta}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\bm{N}^{q_{\zeta+1}}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}},

where we set
T_{\beta_{\zeta+1}}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
as the identity matrix.

By observing each term in Eq. (
26
), we have the following format regarding to parameter matrices

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\\
\bm{N}^{q_{1}}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\end{array}\right\},[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\\
\bm{N}^{q_{2}}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\end{array}\right\},[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}\ldots[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{P}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}\\
\bm{N}^{q_{\zeta+1}}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}\end{array}\right\},

where each
\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}}\\
\bm{N}^{q_{p}}_{k_{p},i_{p}}\end{array}\right\}
comes from the projection or nilpotent parts from the matrix
\bm{X}_{p}
. From Eq.(
3.2
), we obtain the argument matrices
[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1}
corresponding to each occurrence of
[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}
. To align Eq.(
3.2
) with each summand term in Eq.(
26
), we insert an identity matrix both before and after each
[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}
. Accordingly, the full sequence of argument matrices can be written as:

\displaystyle\overbrace{\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I},\ldots,\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I}}^{\mbox{there are $\zeta$ terms of $\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I}$}}.

(34)

Finally, since the complex-valued functions
f
and
\beta_{i}
are commutative, Eq.(
26
) becomes:

\displaystyle T_{f\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\zeta}\beta_{i}}^{\bm{X}_{1},[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\bm{X}_{2},[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1},\ldots,[\bm{X}]1^{\zeta+1},\bm{X}{\zeta+1}}(\overbrace{\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I},\ldots,\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I}}^{\mbox{there are $\zeta$ terms of $\bm{I},[\bm{Y}]^{\zeta}_{1},\bm{I}$}}).

(35)

\hfill\Box

4
GMOI Norm Estimations

The purpose of this section is to provide norm estimates for GMOI. We begin by introducing some index mapping notations. Given a non-negative integer
i
, we use
B(i,\zeta+1)
to represent the binary representation of the integer
i
by
\zeta+1
bits. For example, we have
B(0,2)=00
,
B(1,2)=01
,
B(2,2)=10
, and
B(3,2)=11
. We define the following map for the binary representation
B(i,\zeta+1)
associated to matrices
[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}
as:

\displaystyle\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i,\zeta+1))\rightarrow\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\kappa}q_{\iota_{j}}\bm{e}^{(\zeta+1)}_{\iota_{j}},

(36)

where
\kappa
is the number of one in the binary representation
B(i,\zeta+1)
,
q_{\iota_{j}}
will be the exponent for the nilpotent part of the matrix
\bm{X}_{\iota_{j}}
,
\iota_{j}
is the position (leftmost position is indexed by
1
) of
B(i,\zeta+1)
with value one, and
\bm{e}^{(\zeta+1)}_{\iota_{j}}
is the standard basis vector with the length
\zeta+1
and all zero entries except the value one at the position
\iota_{j}
. For example, we have
\Psi_{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}}(B(0,2))=(0,0)
,
\Psi_{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}}(B(1,2))=(0,q_{2})
,
\Psi_{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}}(B(2,2))=(q_{1},0)
, and
\Psi_{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}}(B(3,2))=(q_{1},q_{2})
.

From GMOI definition given by Eq. (
17
), we can express GMOI by

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=0}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}},

(37)

where
\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}
is a matrix, which can be represented by

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}}-1}

(38)

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))!}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{P}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}

where
\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))
are those non-zero entries of the vector
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\kappa}q_{\iota_{j}}\bm{e}^{(\zeta+1)}_{\iota_{j}}
,

\beta^{\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})
is the partial derivatives with respect to orders
\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))
for the funtion
\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})
,
\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))=1
indicates those
q_{\iota_{j}}
(summand variables) set as
1
,
\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))!
is a product of factorial of those nilpotent exponents
q_{\iota_{j}}
. We still assume that
\bm{Y}_{\zeta+1}=\bm{I}
.

Let us consider the following Example
2
about expressing
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})
by Eq. (
37
).

Example 2

From Eq. (
21
), we have

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}

(39)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\beta(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(-,-,q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{3}!}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(-,q_{2},-)}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{2}!}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},-,-)}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!}\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(-,q_{2},q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{2}!q_{3}!}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},-,q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!q_{3}!}\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},q_{2},-)}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!q_{2}!}\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{P}_{k_{3},i_{3}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\sum\limits_{k_{2}=1}^{K_{2}}\sum\limits_{k_{3}=1}^{K_{3}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{2}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{i_{3}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{3}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{m_{k_{1},i_{1}}-1}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{m_{k_{2},i_{2}}-1}\sum_{q_{3}=1}^{m_{k_{3},i_{3}}-1}\frac{\beta^{(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\lambda_{k_{2}},\lambda_{k_{3}})}{q_{1}!q_{2}!q_{3}!}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \times\bm{N}_{k_{1},i_{1}}^{q_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{N}_{k_{2},i_{2}}^{q_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{N}_{k_{3},i_{3}}^{q_{3}}.

Because we have

\displaystyle B(0,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,0,0)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(0,3))=(0,0,0);

\displaystyle B(1,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,0,1)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(1,3))=(0,0,q_{3});

\displaystyle B(2,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,1,0)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(2,3))=(0,q_{2},0);

\displaystyle B(3,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,1,1)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(3,3))=(0,q_{2},q_{3});

\displaystyle B(4,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,0,0)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B4,3))=(q_{1},0,0);

\displaystyle B(5,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,0,1)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(5,3))=(q_{1},0,q_{3});

\displaystyle B(6,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,1,0)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(6,3))=(q_{1},q_{2},0);

\displaystyle B(7,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,1,1)\rightarrow\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(7,3))=(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}),

(40)

then,

\displaystyle B(0,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,0,0)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(0,3))=\emptyset;

\displaystyle B(1,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,0,1)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(1,3))=(q_{3})\rightarrow m_{k_{3},i_{3}};

\displaystyle B(2,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,1,0)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(2,3))=(q_{2})\rightarrow m_{k_{2},i_{2}};

\displaystyle B(3,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(0,1,1)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(3,3))=(q_{2},q_{3})\rightarrow m_{k_{2},i_{2}},m_{k_{3},i_{3}};

\displaystyle B(4,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,0,0)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B4,3))=(q_{1})\rightarrow m_{k_{1},i_{1}};

\displaystyle B(5,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,0,1)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(5,3))=(q_{1},q_{3})\rightarrow m_{k_{1},i_{1}},m_{k_{3},i_{3}};

\displaystyle B(6,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,1,0)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(6,3))=(q_{1},q_{2})\rightarrow m_{k_{1},i_{1}},m_{k_{2},i_{2}};

\displaystyle B(7,3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,1,1)\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(7,3))=(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})\rightarrow m_{k_{1},i_{1}},m_{k_{2},i_{2}},m_{k_{3},i_{3}}.

(41)

According to Eq. (
38
), we can express
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})
as

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\bm{X}_{3}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{0}+\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{A}_{2}+\bm{A}_{4}+\bm{A}_{3}+\bm{A}_{5}+\bm{A}_{6}+\bm{A}_{7}.

(42)

We recall Lemma 3 in
[
8
]
for the converse triangle inequality for the Frobenius norm, denoted by
\left\|\cdot\right\|
.

Lemma 1

Given
n
matrices
\bm{A}_{1},\bm{A}_{2},\ldots,\bm{A}_{n}
such that
\left\|\bm{A}_{1}\right\|\geq\left\|\bm{A}_{2}\right\|\geq\ldots\geq\left\|\bm{A}_{n}\right\|
, then, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\bm{A}_{i}\right\|\geq\max\left[0,\left\|\bm{A}_{1}\right\|-\sum\limits_{i=2}^{n}\left\|\bm{A}_{i}\right\|\right]

(43)

Theorem
2
will be provided to give the lower bound and the upper bound estimations for the Frobenius norm of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
.

Theorem 2

We have the upper bound for the Frobenius norm of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})=\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=0}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}
, which is given by

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\right\|\leq\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=0}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{up},

(44)

where
\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{up}
are upper bounds for the Frobenius norm of the matrix
\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}
, which is defined by

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{up}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{K_{\varsigma},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}\sum\limits_{i_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{\alpha_{k_{\varsigma}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}

(45)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}}-1}

\displaystyle\left[\max\limits_{\lambda_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\frac{\beta^{(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))!}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\left\|\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\right\|\left\|\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\right\|\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\left\|\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\right\|\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}

On the other hand, we have the lower bound for the Frobenius norm of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
, which is given by

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\right\|\geq\max\left[0,\left\|\bm{A}_{\sigma(0)}\right\|-\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\left\|\bm{A}_{\sigma(i^{\prime})}\right\|\right],

(46)

where
\sigma
is the permutation of matrices
\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}
for
i^{\prime}=0,1,\ldots,2^{\zeta+1}-1
such that
\left\|\bm{A}_{\sigma(0)}\right\|\geq\left\|\bm{A}_{\sigma(1)}\right\|\geq\ldots\geq\left\|\bm{A}_{\sigma(2^{\zeta+1}-1)}\right\|
.

Further, if we have
\left[\min\limits_{\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{\zeta+1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\beta(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\zeta+1})\right|\right]\left\|\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{\zeta}\bm{Y}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|\geq\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|
, the lower bound for the Frobenius norm of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}}(\bm{Y})
can be expressed by

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\right\|\geq\left[\min\limits_{\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{\zeta+1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\beta(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\zeta+1})\right|\right]\left\|\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{\zeta}\bm{Y}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|-\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|.

(47)

Proof:

From Eq. (
37
), we have

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=0}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}},

(48)

and we immediate have the following by the triangle inequality of Frobenius norm:

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=0}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|.

(49)

From the
\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}
expression given by Eq. (
38
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}}-1}\right.

(50)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\beta^{(\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))!}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{P}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{K_{\varsigma},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}\sum\limits_{i_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{\alpha_{k_{\varsigma}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}}-1}

\displaystyle\left[\max\limits_{\lambda_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\frac{\beta^{(\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))!}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\times\left\|\sum\limits_{k_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{K_{\varsigma},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}\sum\limits_{i_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{\alpha_{k_{\varsigma}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\bm{P}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq_{1}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{K_{\varsigma},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}\sum\limits_{i_{\varsigma}=1,\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}^{\alpha_{k_{\varsigma}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\mbox{for}\penalty\ \varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))=1}^{m_{k_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))},i_{\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}}-1}

\displaystyle\left[\max\limits_{\lambda_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\frac{\beta^{(\Psi_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))}(\lambda_{k_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{k_{\zeta+1}})}{\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))!}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma=\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\left\|\bm{N}^{q_{\varsigma}}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}\right\|\left\|\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\right\|\\
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1))),\bm{Z}_{\varsigma}=\left\|\bm{Y}_{\varsigma}\right\|\end{subarray}}^{\zeta+1}\bm{Z}_{\varsigma},

where we apply
\sum\limits_{k_{\varsigma}=1}^{K_{\varsigma}}\sum\limits_{i_{\varsigma}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{\varsigma}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\bm{P}_{k_{\varsigma},i_{\varsigma}}=\bm{I}
for
\varsigma\neq\mbox{Ind}(\tilde{\Psi}_{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{\zeta+1}}(B(i^{\prime},\zeta+1)))
, and the triangle inequality of Frobenius norm again in
\leq_{1}
. Therefore, we have the upper bound for
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
as the upper bound shown by Eq. (
50
) is identical to
\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|_{up}
.

For the lower bound of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
, we have Eq. (
46
) immediatedly from Lemma
1
.

If we have
\left[\min\limits_{\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{\zeta+1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\beta(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\zeta+1})\right|\right]\left\|\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{\zeta}\bm{Y}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|\geq\sum\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\left\|\bm{A}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|
and Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})\right\|

(51)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}_{0}\right\|-(\left\|\bm{A}_{1}\right\|+\left\|\bm{A}_{2}\right\|+\ldots+\left\|\bm{A}_{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\right\|)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left[\min\limits_{\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{\zeta+1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\beta(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\zeta+1})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\times\left\|\sum\limits_{k_{1}=\ldots=k_{\zeta+1}=1}^{K_{1},\ldots,K_{\zeta+1}}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=\ldots=i_{\zeta+1}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{1}}^{(\mathrm{G})},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{\zeta+1}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\bm{P}_{k_{1},i_{1}}\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{P}_{k_{2},i_{2}}\bm{Y}_{2}\ldots\bm{Y}_{\zeta}\bm{P}_{k_{\zeta+1},i_{\zeta+1}}\right\|

\displaystyle-(\left\|\bm{A}_{1}\right\|+\left\|\bm{A}_{2}\right\|+\ldots+\left\|\bm{A}_{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\right\|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\min\limits_{\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{1}},\ldots,\lambda_{\zeta+1}\in\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}}\left|\beta(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{\zeta+1})\right|\right]\left\|\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}=1}^{\zeta}\bm{Y}_{i^{\prime}}\right\|

\displaystyle-(\left\|\bm{A}_{1}\right\|+\left\|\bm{A}_{2}\right\|+\ldots+\left\|\bm{A}_{2^{\zeta+1}-1}\right\|),

which is the lower bound of
T_{\beta}^{\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{\zeta+1}}(\bm{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bm{Y}_{\zeta})
given by Eq. (
47
).

\hfill\Box

5
Perturbation Formula and Lipschitz Estimations

5.1
Perturbation Formula and Nilpotent Part Difference Characterization

In this section, the perturbation formula of GMOI is derived. We begin by defining several new notations for later perturbation formula simpler presentation. We have

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{k_{c},i_{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{P}_{k_{c},i_{c}}\\
\bm{N}_{k_{c},i_{c}}^{q_{c}}\end{cases};

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{k_{d},i_{d}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{P}_{k_{d},i_{d}}\\
\bm{N}_{k_{d},i_{d}}^{q_{d}}\end{cases}.

(52)

where
c,d
are indices for the matrices
\bm{C}
and
\bm{D}
. Also, we set

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}}\\
\bm{N}_{k_{p},i_{p}}^{q_{p}}\end{cases};

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{k_{p^{\prime}},i_{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{P}_{k_{p^{\prime}},i_{p^{\prime}}}\\
\bm{N}_{k_{p^{\prime}},i_{p^{\prime}}}^{q_{p^{\prime}}}\end{cases},

(53)

where
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
are indices for the matrices
\bm{X}_{p}
and
\bm{X}_{p^{\prime}}
. We use the following summation notation:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}},\mbox{\penalty\ \penalty\ if $\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}}=\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}}$},\\
\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{(\mathrm{G})}}\sum\limits_{q_{p}=1}^{m_{k_{p},i_{p}}}\bm{N}_{k_{p},i_{p}}^{q_{p}},\mbox{\penalty\ \penalty\ if $\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}}=\bm{N}_{k_{p},i_{p}}^{q_{p}}$},\end{cases}

(54)

therefore, if we write
\sum\limits_{\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}},p\in[p_{1},p_{2}]}
, which can be expressed by

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}},p\in[p_{1},p_{2}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\bm{S}_{k_{p_{1}},i_{p_{1}}}}\sum\limits_{\bm{S}_{k_{p_{2}},i_{p_{2}}}}\ldots\sum\limits_{\bm{S}_{k_{p_{2}},i_{p_{2}}}}

(55)

where
[p_{1},p_{2}]
are positive integers from
p_{1},p_{1}+1,\ldots,p_{2}
. Note that the expression given by Eq. (
55
) will include all summation results for all binary combinations of
\bm{S}_{k_{p},i_{p}}
. Hence, there are
2^{p_{2}-p_{1}+1}
summation results given by Eq. (
55
).

Given any differentiable function
f:\mathbb{C}^{\zeta}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we use the following notation to represent partial derivatives with respect to different arguments of the function
f
and its normalization with respect to differentiation orders:

\displaystyle\frac{f^{(q_{1},q_{2},\ldots,q_{\zeta})}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{\zeta})}{q_{1}!q_{2}!\ldots,q_{\zeta}!}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f^{([q]_{1}^{q_{\zeta}})}([\lambda]_{1}^{\zeta})}{([q!]_{1}^{q_{\zeta}})}

(56)

The Lemma
2
will be provided to show that the
k
-order divided difference identity will be valid for the function and its partial derivatives.

Lemma 2

Let
f:\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}
be a function of class
C^{k+1+r}
. Let

D^{(r)}f=\frac{\partial^{r}f}{\partial x_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial x_{i_{r}}}

be an
r
-th order partial derivative of
f
. Then the divided difference identity

D^{(r)}f^{[k]}(\dots,\lambda_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)-D^{(r)}f^{[k]}(\dots,\mu_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)=(\lambda_{\alpha_{j}}-\mu_{\alpha_{j}})D^{(r)}f^{[k+1]}(\dots,\lambda_{\alpha_{j}},\mu_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)

holds provided that the divided difference is taken with respect to a variable independent of
x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{r}}
.

Proof:

Assume
f\in C^{k+1+r}(\mathbb{C}^{n})
, so that all relevant derivatives and divided differences are well-defined and smooth.

Let
g=D^{(r)}f=\frac{\partial^{r}f}{\partial x_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial x_{i_{r}}}
. Because differentiation and divided difference operators commute when they act on independent variables, we have:

D^{(r)}f^{[k]}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k})=\left(D^{(r)}f\right)^{[k]}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{k})

Now, apply the standard first-order divided difference identity to the function
g
:

g^{[k]}(\dots,\lambda_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)-g^{[k]}(\dots,\mu_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)=(\lambda_{\alpha_{j}}-\mu_{\alpha_{j}})g^{[k+1]}(\dots,\lambda_{\alpha_{j}},\mu_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)

Substituting back
g=D^{(r)}f
, we obtain:

D^{(r)}f^{[k]}(\dots,\lambda_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)-D^{(r)}f^{[k]}(\dots,\mu_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)=(\lambda_{\alpha_{j}}-\mu_{\alpha_{j}})D^{(r)}f^{[k+1]}(\dots,\lambda_{\alpha_{j}},\mu_{\alpha_{j}},\dots)

which completes the proof.

\hfill\Box

We are ready to present Theorem
3
about GMOI perturbation formula.

Theorem 3

Given matrices
\bm{X}_{p}
decomposed as:

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\lambda_{k_{p}}\bm{P}_{k_{p},i_{p}}+\sum\limits_{k_{p}=1}^{K_{p}}\sum\limits_{i_{p}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{p}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\bm{N}_{k_{p},i_{p}},

(57)

where
p=1,2,\ldots,\zeta
, and matrices
\bm{C},\bm{D}
decomposed as:

\displaystyle\bm{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{c}=1}^{K_{c}}\sum\limits_{i_{c}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{c}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\lambda_{k_{c}}\bm{P}_{k_{c},i_{c}}+\sum\limits_{k_{c}=1}^{K_{c}}\sum\limits_{i_{c}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{c}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\bm{N}_{k_{c},i_{c}},

\displaystyle\bm{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\sum\limits_{i_{d}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{d}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\lambda_{k_{d}}\bm{P}_{k_{d},i_{d}}+\sum\limits_{k_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\sum\limits_{i_{d}=1}^{\alpha_{k_{d}}^{\mathrm{G}}}\bm{N}_{k_{d},i_{d}}.

(58)

We have the following formula for GMOI:

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C},\bm{D},[\bm{X}]_{j}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})=

(59)

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{I}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,P}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta}))\Bigg)_{II}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{III}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{IV}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{V}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,P}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{VI}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{VII}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{P},\bm{D}_{P},\bm{X}_{j,N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Bigg(T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})\Bigg)_{VIII}+\mathfrak{X}^{\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,N},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C},[\bm{X}]_{j}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})-T_{\beta^{[\zeta]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{D},[\bm{X}]_{j}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})+\bar{\mathfrak{X}}([\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C},\bm{D},[\bm{X}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,P},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T_{\beta^{[\zeta+1]}}^{[\bm{X}]_{1}^{j-2},\bm{X}_{j-1,P},\bm{C}_{N},\bm{D}_{N},\bm{X}_{j,N},[\bm{X}]_{j+1}^{\zeta}}([\bm{Y}]_{1}^{j-1},\bm{C}-\bm{D},[\bm{Y}]_{j}^{\zeta})

\displaystyle+
